     115             :  * @page page_iface_wl_registry wl_registry
     116             :  * @section page_iface_wl_registry_desc Description
     117             :  *
     118             :  * The singleton global registry object.  The server has a number of
     119             :  * global objects that are available to all clients.  These objects
     120             :  * typically represent an actual object in the server (for example,
     121             :  * an input device) or they are singleton objects that provide
     122             :  * extension functionality.
     123             :  *
     124             :  * When a client creates a registry object, the registry object
     125             :  * will emit a global event for each global currently in the
     126             :  * registry.  Globals come and go as a result of device or
     127             :  * monitor hotplugs, reconfiguration or other events, and the
     128             :  * registry will send out global and global_remove events to
     129             :  * keep the client up to date with the changes.  To mark the end
     130             :  * of the initial burst of events, the client can use the
     131             :  * wl_display.sync request immediately after calling
     132             :  * wl_display.get_registry.
     133             :  *
     134             :  * A client can bind to a global object by using the bind
     135             :  * request.  This creates a client-side handle that lets the object
     136             :  * emit events to the client and lets the client invoke requests on
     137             :  * the object.
     138             :  * @section page_iface_wl_registry_api API
     139             :  * See @ref iface_wl_registry.
     140             :  */

     275             : #ifndef WL_BUFFER_INTERFACE
     276             : #define WL_BUFFER_INTERFACE
     277             : /**
     278             :  * @page page_iface_wl_buffer wl_buffer
     279             :  * @section page_iface_wl_buffer_desc Description
     280             :  *
     281             :  * A buffer provides the content for a wl_surface. Buffers are
     282             :  * created through factory interfaces such as wl_shm, wp_linux_buffer_params
     283             :  * (from the linux-dmabuf protocol extension) or similar. It has a width and
     284             :  * a height and can be attached to a wl_surface, but the mechanism by which a
     285             :  * client provides and updates the contents is defined by the buffer factory
     286             :  * interface.
     287             :  *
     288             :  * Color channels are assumed to be electrical rather than optical (in other
     289             :  * words, encoded with a transfer function) unless otherwise specified. If
     290             :  * the buffer uses a format that has an alpha channel, the alpha channel is
     291             :  * assumed to be premultiplied into the electrical color channel values
     292             :  * (after transfer function encoding) unless otherwise specified.
     293             :  *
     294             :  * Note, because wl_buffer objects are created from multiple independent
     295             :  * factory interfaces, the wl_buffer interface is frozen at version 1.
     296             :  * @section page_iface_wl_buffer_api API
     297             :  * See @ref iface_wl_buffer.
     298             :  */
     299             : /**
     300             :  * @defgroup iface_wl_buffer The wl_buffer interface
     301             :  *
     302             :  * A buffer provides the content for a wl_surface. Buffers are
     303             :  * created through factory interfaces such as wl_shm, wp_linux_buffer_params
     304             :  * (from the linux-dmabuf protocol extension) or similar. It has a width and
     305             :  * a height and can be attached to a wl_surface, but the mechanism by which a
     306             :  * client provides and updates the contents is defined by the buffer factory
     307             :  * interface.
     308             :  *
     309             :  * Color channels are assumed to be electrical rather than optical (in other
     310             :  * words, encoded with a transfer function) unless otherwise specified. If
     311             :  * the buffer uses a format that has an alpha channel, the alpha channel is
     312             :  * assumed to be premultiplied into the electrical color channel values
     313             :  * (after transfer function encoding) unless otherwise specified.
     314             :  *
     315             :  * Note, because wl_buffer objects are created from multiple independent
     316             :  * factory interfaces, the wl_buffer interface is frozen at version 1.

     465             : /**
     466             :  * @page page_iface_wl_shell_surface wl_shell_surface
     467             :  * @section page_iface_wl_shell_surface_desc Description
     468             :  *
     469             :  * An interface that may be implemented by a wl_surface, for
     470             :  * implementations that provide a desktop-style user interface.
     471             :  *
     472             :  * It provides requests to treat surfaces like toplevel, fullscreen
     473             :  * or popup windows, move, resize or maximize them, associate
     474             :  * metadata like title and class, etc.
     475             :  *
     476             :  * On the server side the object is automatically destroyed when
     477             :  * the related wl_surface is destroyed. On the client side,
     478             :  * wl_shell_surface_destroy() must be called before destroying
     479             :  * the wl_surface object.
     480             :  * @section page_iface_wl_shell_surface_api API
     481             :  * See @ref iface_wl_shell_surface.
     482             :  */
     483             : /**
     484             :  * @defgroup iface_wl_shell_surface The wl_shell_surface interface
     485             :  *
     486             :  * An interface that may be implemented by a wl_surface, for
     487             :  * implementations that provide a desktop-style user interface.
     488             :  *
     489             :  * It provides requests to treat surfaces like toplevel, fullscreen
     490             :  * or popup windows, move, resize or maximize them, associate
     491             :  * metadata like title and class, etc.
     492             :  *
     493             :  * On the server side the object is automatically destroyed when
     494             :  * the related wl_surface is destroyed. On the client side,
     495             :  * wl_shell_surface_destroy() must be called before destroying
     496             :  * the wl_surface object.
     497             :  */
     498             : extern const struct wl_interface wl_shell_surface_interface;
     499             : #endif
     500             : #ifndef WL_SURFACE_INTERFACE
     501             : #define WL_SURFACE_INTERFACE
     502             : /**
     503             :  * @page page_iface_wl_surface wl_surface
     504             :  * @section page_iface_wl_surface_desc Description
     505             :  *
     506             :  * A surface is a rectangular area that may be displayed on zero
     507             :  * or more outputs, and shown any number of times at the compositor's
     508             :  * discretion. They can present wl_buffers, receive user input, and
     509             :  * define a local coordinate system.
     510             :  *
     511             :  * The size of a surface (and relative positions on it) is described
     512             :  * in surface-local coordinates, which may differ from the buffer
     513             :  * coordinates of the pixel content, in case a buffer_transform
     514             :  * or a buffer_scale is used.
     515             :  *
     516             :  * A surface without a "role" is fairly useless: a compositor does
     517             :  * not know where, when or how to present it. The role is the
     518             :  * purpose of a wl_surface. Examples of roles are a cursor for a
     519             :  * pointer (as set by wl_pointer.set_cursor), a drag icon
     520             :  * (wl_data_device.start_drag), a sub-surface
     521             :  * (wl_subcompositor.get_subsurface), and a window as defined by a
     522             :  * shell protocol (e.g. wl_shell.get_shell_surface).
     523             :  *
     524             :  * A surface can have only one role at a time. Initially a
     525             :  * wl_surface does not have a role. Once a wl_surface is given a
     526             :  * role, it is set permanently for the whole lifetime of the
     527             :  * wl_surface object. Giving the current role again is allowed,
     528             :  * unless explicitly forbidden by the relevant interface
     529             :  * specification.
     530             :  *
     531             :  * Surface roles are given by requests in other interfaces such as
     532             :  * wl_pointer.set_cursor. The request should explicitly mention
     533             :  * that this request gives a role to a wl_surface. Often, this
     534             :  * request also creates a new protocol object that represents the
     535             :  * role and adds additional functionality to wl_surface. When a
     536             :  * client wants to destroy a wl_surface, they must destroy this role
     537             :  * object before the wl_surface, otherwise a defunct_role_object error is
     538             :  * sent.
     539             :  *
     540             :  * Destroying the role object does not remove the role from the
     541             :  * wl_surface, but it may stop the wl_surface from "playing the role".
     542             :  * For instance, if a wl_subsurface object is destroyed, the wl_surface
     543             :  * it was created for will be unmapped and forget its position and
     544             :  * z-order. It is allowed to create a wl_subsurface for the same
     545             :  * wl_surface again, but it is not allowed to use the wl_surface as
     546             :  * a cursor (cursor is a different role than sub-surface, and role
     547             :  * switching is not allowed).
     548             :  * @section page_iface_wl_surface_api API
     549             :  * See @ref iface_wl_surface.
     550             :  */
     551             : /**
     552             :  * @defgroup iface_wl_surface The wl_surface interface
     553             :  *
     554             :  * A surface is a rectangular area that may be displayed on zero
     555             :  * or more outputs, and shown any number of times at the compositor's
     556             :  * discretion. They can present wl_buffers, receive user input, and
     557             :  * define a local coordinate system.
     558             :  *
     559             :  * The size of a surface (and relative positions on it) is described
     560             :  * in surface-local coordinates, which may differ from the buffer
     561             :  * coordinates of the pixel content, in case a buffer_transform
     562             :  * or a buffer_scale is used.
     563             :  *
     564             :  * A surface without a "role" is fairly useless: a compositor does
     565             :  * not know where, when or how to present it. The role is the
     566             :  * purpose of a wl_surface. Examples of roles are a cursor for a
     567             :  * pointer (as set by wl_pointer.set_cursor), a drag icon
     568             :  * (wl_data_device.start_drag), a sub-surface
     569             :  * (wl_subcompositor.get_subsurface), and a window as defined by a
     570             :  * shell protocol (e.g. wl_shell.get_shell_surface).
     571             :  *
     572             :  * A surface can have only one role at a time. Initially a
     573             :  * wl_surface does not have a role. Once a wl_surface is given a
     574             :  * role, it is set permanently for the whole lifetime of the
     575             :  * wl_surface object. Giving the current role again is allowed,
     576             :  * unless explicitly forbidden by the relevant interface
     577             :  * specification.
     578             :  *
     579             :  * Surface roles are given by requests in other interfaces such as
     580             :  * wl_pointer.set_cursor. The request should explicitly mention
     581             :  * that this request gives a role to a wl_surface. Often, this
     582             :  * request also creates a new protocol object that represents the
     583             :  * role and adds additional functionality to wl_surface. When a
     584             :  * client wants to destroy a wl_surface, they must destroy this role
     585             :  * object before the wl_surface, otherwise a defunct_role_object error is
     586             :  * sent.
     587             :  *
     588             :  * Destroying the role object does not remove the role from the
     589             :  * wl_surface, but it may stop the wl_surface from "playing the role".
     590             :  * For instance, if a wl_subsurface object is destroyed, the wl_surface
     591             :  * it was created for will be unmapped and forget its position and
     592             :  * z-order. It is allowed to create a wl_subsurface for the same
     593             :  * wl_surface again, but it is not allowed to use the wl_surface as
     594             :  * a cursor (cursor is a different role than sub-surface, and role
     595             :  * switching is not allowed).
     596             :  */
     597             : extern const struct wl_interface wl_surface_interface;
     598             : #endif
     599             : #ifndef WL_SEAT_INTERFACE
     600             : #define WL_SEAT_INTERFACE
     601             : /**
     602             :  * @page page_iface_wl_seat wl_seat

     606             :  * object is published as a global during start up, or when such a
     607             :  * device is hot plugged.  A seat typically has a pointer and
     608             :  * maintains a keyboard focus and a pointer focus.

     610             :  * See @ref iface_wl_seat.
     611             :  */
     612             : /**
     613             :  * @defgroup iface_wl_seat The wl_seat interface
     614             :  *
     615             :  * A seat is a group of keyboards, pointer and touch devices. This
     616             :  * object is published as a global during start up, or when such a
     617             :  * device is hot plugged.  A seat typically has a pointer and
     618             :  * maintains a keyboard focus and a pointer focus.
     619             :  */
     620             : extern const struct wl_interface wl_seat_interface;
     621             : #endif
     622             : #ifndef WL_POINTER_INTERFACE
     623             : #define WL_POINTER_INTERFACE
     624             : /**
     625             :  * @page page_iface_wl_pointer wl_pointer

     639             : /**
     640             :  * @defgroup iface_wl_pointer The wl_pointer interface
     641             :  *
     642             :  * The wl_pointer interface represents one or more input devices,
     643             :  * such as mice, which control the pointer location and pointer_focus
     644             :  * of a seat.
     645             :  *
     646             :  * The wl_pointer interface generates motion, enter and leave
     647             :  * events for the surfaces that the pointer is located over,
     648             :  * and button and axis events for button presses, button releases
     649             :  * and scrolling.
     650             :  */
     651             : extern const struct wl_interface wl_pointer_interface;
     652             : #endif
     653             : #ifndef WL_KEYBOARD_INTERFACE
     654             : #define WL_KEYBOARD_INTERFACE
     655             : /**
     656             :  * @page page_iface_wl_keyboard wl_keyboard
     657             :  * @section page_iface_wl_keyboard_desc Description
     658             :  *
     659             :  * The wl_keyboard interface represents one or more keyboards
     660             :  * associated with a seat.
     661             :  *
     662             :  * Each wl_keyboard has the following logical state:
     663             :  *
     664             :  * - an active surface (possibly null),
     665             :  * - the keys currently logically down,
     666             :  * - the active modifiers,
     667             :  * - the active group.
     668             :  *
     669             :  * By default, the active surface is null, the keys currently logically down
     670             :  * are empty, the active modifiers and the active group are 0.
     671             :  * @section page_iface_wl_keyboard_api API
     672             :  * See @ref iface_wl_keyboard.
     673             :  */
     674             : /**
     675             :  * @defgroup iface_wl_keyboard The wl_keyboard interface
     676             :  *
     677             :  * The wl_keyboard interface represents one or more keyboards
     678             :  * associated with a seat.
     679             :  *
     680             :  * Each wl_keyboard has the following logical state:
     681             :  *
     682             :  * - an active surface (possibly null),
     683             :  * - the keys currently logically down,
     684             :  * - the active modifiers,
     685             :  * - the active group.
     686             :  *
     687             :  * By default, the active surface is null, the keys currently logically down
     688             :  * are empty, the active modifiers and the active group are 0.
     689             :  */

     695             :  * @page page_iface_wl_touch wl_touch

     699             :  * associated with a seat.

     704             :  * and ending with an up event. Events relating to the same
     705             :  * contact point can be identified by the ID of the sequence.
     706             :  * @section page_iface_wl_touch_api API
     707             :  * See @ref iface_wl_touch.
     708             :  */

     716             :  * For each contact, a series of events is generated, starting
     717             :  * with a down event, followed by zero or more motion events,
     718             :  * and ending with an up event. Events relating to the same
     719             :  * contact point can be identified by the ID of the sequence.
     720             :  */
     721             : extern const struct wl_interface wl_touch_interface;
     722             : #endif
     723             : #ifndef WL_OUTPUT_INTERFACE

     725             : /**
     726             :  * @page page_iface_wl_output wl_output

     735             :  * @section page_iface_wl_output_api API
     736             :  * See @ref iface_wl_output.
     737             :  */
     738             : /**
     739             :  * @defgroup iface_wl_output The wl_output interface
     740             :  *
     741             :  * An output describes part of the compositor geometry.  The
     742             :  * compositor works in the 'compositor coordinate system' and an
     743             :  * output corresponds to a rectangular area in that space that is
     744             :  * actually visible.  This typically corresponds to a monitor that
     745             :  * displays part of the compositor space.  This object is published
     746             :  * as global during start up, or when a monitor is hotplugged.
     747             :  */

     772             : #endif
     773             : #ifndef WL_SUBCOMPOSITOR_INTERFACE
     774             : #define WL_SUBCOMPOSITOR_INTERFACE
     775             : /**
     776             :  * @page page_iface_wl_subcompositor wl_subcompositor
     777             :  * @section page_iface_wl_subcompositor_desc Description
     778             :  *
     779             :  * The global interface exposing sub-surface compositing capabilities.
     780             :  * A wl_surface, that has sub-surfaces associated, is called the
     781             :  * parent surface. Sub-surfaces can be arbitrarily nested and create
     782             :  * a tree of sub-surfaces.
     783             :  *
     784             :  * The root surface in a tree of sub-surfaces is the main
     785             :  * surface. The main surface cannot be a sub-surface, because
     786             :  * sub-surfaces must always have a parent.
     787             :  *
     788             :  * A main surface with its sub-surfaces forms a (compound) window.
     789             :  * For window management purposes, this set of wl_surface objects is
     790             :  * to be considered as a single window, and it should also behave as
     791             :  * such.
     792             :  *
     793             :  * The aim of sub-surfaces is to offload some of the compositing work
     794             :  * within a window from clients to the compositor. A prime example is
     795             :  * a video player with decorations and video in separate wl_surface
     796             :  * objects. This should allow the compositor to pass YUV video buffer
     797             :  * processing to dedicated overlay hardware when possible.
     798             :  * @section page_iface_wl_subcompositor_api API
     799             :  * See @ref iface_wl_subcompositor.
     800             :  */
     801             : /**
     802             :  * @defgroup iface_wl_subcompositor The wl_subcompositor interface
     803             :  *
     804             :  * The global interface exposing sub-surface compositing capabilities.
     805             :  * A wl_surface, that has sub-surfaces associated, is called the
     806             :  * parent surface. Sub-surfaces can be arbitrarily nested and create
     807             :  * a tree of sub-surfaces.
     808             :  *
     809             :  * The root surface in a tree of sub-surfaces is the main
     810             :  * surface. The main surface cannot be a sub-surface, because
     811             :  * sub-surfaces must always have a parent.
     812             :  *
     813             :  * A main surface with its sub-surfaces forms a (compound) window.
     814             :  * For window management purposes, this set of wl_surface objects is
     815             :  * to be considered as a single window, and it should also behave as
     816             :  * such.
     817             :  *
     818             :  * The aim of sub-surfaces is to offload some of the compositing work
     819             :  * within a window from clients to the compositor. A prime example is
     820             :  * a video player with decorations and video in separate wl_surface
     821             :  * objects. This should allow the compositor to pass YUV video buffer
     822             :  * processing to dedicated overlay hardware when possible.
     823             :  */
     824             : extern const struct wl_interface wl_subcompositor_interface;
     825             : #endif
     826             : #ifndef WL_SUBSURFACE_INTERFACE
     827             : #define WL_SUBSURFACE_INTERFACE
     828             : /**
     829             :  * @page page_iface_wl_subsurface wl_subsurface
     830             :  * @section page_iface_wl_subsurface_desc Description
     831             :  *
     832             :  * An additional interface to a wl_surface object, which has been
     833             :  * made a sub-surface. A sub-surface has one parent surface. A
     834             :  * sub-surface's size and position are not limited to that of the parent.
     835             :  * Particularly, a sub-surface is not automatically clipped to its
     836             :  * parent's area.
     837             :  *
     838             :  * A sub-surface becomes mapped, when a non-NULL wl_buffer is applied
     839             :  * and the parent surface is mapped. The order of which one happens
     840             :  * first is irrelevant. A sub-surface is hidden if the parent becomes
     841             :  * hidden, or if a NULL wl_buffer is applied. These rules apply
     842             :  * recursively through the tree of surfaces.
     843             :  *
     844             :  * The behaviour of a wl_surface.commit request on a sub-surface
     845             :  * depends on the sub-surface's mode. The possible modes are
     846             :  * synchronized and desynchronized, see methods
     847             :  * wl_subsurface.set_sync and wl_subsurface.set_desync. Synchronized
     848             :  * mode caches the wl_surface state to be applied when the parent's
     849             :  * state gets applied, and desynchronized mode applies the pending
     850             :  * wl_surface state directly. A sub-surface is initially in the
     851             :  * synchronized mode.
     852             :  *
     853             :  * Sub-surfaces also have another kind of state, which is managed by
     854             :  * wl_subsurface requests, as opposed to wl_surface requests. This
     855             :  * state includes the sub-surface position relative to the parent
     856             :  * surface (wl_subsurface.set_position), and the stacking order of
     857             :  * the parent and its sub-surfaces (wl_subsurface.place_above and
     858             :  * .place_below). This state is applied when the parent surface's
     859             :  * wl_surface state is applied, regardless of the sub-surface's mode.
     860             :  * As the exception, set_sync and set_desync are effective immediately.
     861             :  *
     862             :  * The main surface can be thought to be always in desynchronized mode,
     863             :  * since it does not have a parent in the sub-surfaces sense.
     864             :  *
     865             :  * Even if a sub-surface is in desynchronized mode, it will behave as
     866             :  * in synchronized mode, if its parent surface behaves as in
     867             :  * synchronized mode. This rule is applied recursively throughout the
     868             :  * tree of surfaces. This means, that one can set a sub-surface into
     869             :  * synchronized mode, and then assume that all its child and grand-child
     870             :  * sub-surfaces are synchronized, too, without explicitly setting them.
     871             :  *
     872             :  * Destroying a sub-surface takes effect immediately. If you need to
     873             :  * synchronize the removal of a sub-surface to the parent surface update,
     874             :  * unmap the sub-surface first by attaching a NULL wl_buffer, update parent,
     875             :  * and then destroy the sub-surface.
     876             :  *
     877             :  * If the parent wl_surface object is destroyed, the sub-surface is
     878             :  * unmapped.
     879             :  *
     880             :  * A sub-surface never has the keyboard focus of any seat.
     881             :  *
     882             :  * The wl_surface.offset request is ignored: clients must use set_position
     883             :  * instead to move the sub-surface.
     884             :  * @section page_iface_wl_subsurface_api API
     885             :  * See @ref iface_wl_subsurface.
     886             :  */
     887             : /**
     888             :  * @defgroup iface_wl_subsurface The wl_subsurface interface
     889             :  *
     890             :  * An additional interface to a wl_surface object, which has been
     891             :  * made a sub-surface. A sub-surface has one parent surface. A
     892             :  * sub-surface's size and position are not limited to that of the parent.
     893             :  * Particularly, a sub-surface is not automatically clipped to its
     894             :  * parent's area.
     895             :  *
     896             :  * A sub-surface becomes mapped, when a non-NULL wl_buffer is applied
     897             :  * and the parent surface is mapped. The order of which one happens
     898             :  * first is irrelevant. A sub-surface is hidden if the parent becomes
     899             :  * hidden, or if a NULL wl_buffer is applied. These rules apply
     900             :  * recursively through the tree of surfaces.
     901             :  *
     902             :  * The behaviour of a wl_surface.commit request on a sub-surface
     903             :  * depends on the sub-surface's mode. The possible modes are
     904             :  * synchronized and desynchronized, see methods
     905             :  * wl_subsurface.set_sync and wl_subsurface.set_desync. Synchronized
     906             :  * mode caches the wl_surface state to be applied when the parent's
     907             :  * state gets applied, and desynchronized mode applies the pending
     908             :  * wl_surface state directly. A sub-surface is initially in the
     909             :  * synchronized mode.
     910             :  *
     911             :  * Sub-surfaces also have another kind of state, which is managed by
     912             :  * wl_subsurface requests, as opposed to wl_surface requests. This
     913             :  * state includes the sub-surface position relative to the parent
     914             :  * surface (wl_subsurface.set_position), and the stacking order of
     915             :  * the parent and its sub-surfaces (wl_subsurface.place_above and
     916             :  * .place_below). This state is applied when the parent surface's
     917             :  * wl_surface state is applied, regardless of the sub-surface's mode.
     918             :  * As the exception, set_sync and set_desync are effective immediately.
     919             :  *
     920             :  * The main surface can be thought to be always in desynchronized mode,
     921             :  * since it does not have a parent in the sub-surfaces sense.
     922             :  *
     923             :  * Even if a sub-surface is in desynchronized mode, it will behave as
     924             :  * in synchronized mode, if its parent surface behaves as in
     925             :  * synchronized mode. This rule is applied recursively throughout the
     926             :  * tree of surfaces. This means, that one can set a sub-surface into
     927             :  * synchronized mode, and then assume that all its child and grand-child
     928             :  * sub-surfaces are synchronized, too, without explicitly setting them.
     929             :  *
     930             :  * Destroying a sub-surface takes effect immediately. If you need to
     931             :  * synchronize the removal of a sub-surface to the parent surface update,
     932             :  * unmap the sub-surface first by attaching a NULL wl_buffer, update parent,
     933             :  * and then destroy the sub-surface.
     934             :  *
     935             :  * If the parent wl_surface object is destroyed, the sub-surface is
     936             :  * unmapped.
     937             :  *
     938             :  * A sub-surface never has the keyboard focus of any seat.
     939             :  *
     940             :  * The wl_surface.offset request is ignored: clients must use set_position

    1088             :  * The sync request asks the server to emit the 'done' event

    1463             :  * This request will cause the server to remap the backing memory
    1464             :  * for the pool from the file descriptor passed when the pool was
    1465             :  * created, but using the new size.  This request can only be
    1466             :  * used to make the pool bigger.
    1467             :  *
    1468             :  * This request only changes the amount of bytes that are mmapped
    1469             :  * by the server and does not touch the file corresponding to the
    1470             :  * file descriptor passed at creation time. It is the client's
    1471             :  * responsibility to ensure that the file is at least as big as
    1472             :  * the new pool size.
    1473             :  */
    1474             : static inline void
    1475             : wl_shm_pool_resize(struct wl_shm_pool *wl_shm_pool, int32_t size)
    1476             : {
    1477             :         wl_proxy_marshal_flags((struct wl_proxy *) wl_shm_pool,
    1478             :                          WL_SHM_POOL_RESIZE, NULL, wl_proxy_get_version((struct wl_proxy *) wl_shm_pool), 0, size);
    1479             : }

    1507             : /**
    1508             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_shm
    1509             :  * pixel formats
    1510             :  *
    1511             :  * This describes the memory layout of an individual pixel.
    1512             :  *
    1513             :  * All renderers should support argb8888 and xrgb8888 but any other
    1514             :  * formats are optional and may not be supported by the particular
    1515             :  * renderer in use.
    1516             :  *
    1517             :  * The drm format codes match the macros defined in drm_fourcc.h, except
    1518             :  * argb8888 and xrgb8888. The formats actually supported by the compositor
    1519             :  * will be reported by the format event.
    1520             :  *
    1521             :  * For all wl_shm formats and unless specified in another protocol
    1522             :  * extension, pre-multiplied alpha is used for pixel values.
    1523             :  */

    2100             :          *

    2202             : struct wl_data_offer_listener {
    2203             :         /**
    2204             :          * advertise offered mime type
    2205             :          *
    2206             :          * Sent immediately after creating the wl_data_offer object. One
    2207             :          * event per offered mime type.
    2208             :          * @param mime_type offered mime type
    2209             :          */
    2210             :         void (*offer)(void *data,
    2211             :                       struct wl_data_offer *wl_data_offer,
    2212             :                       const char *mime_type);
    2213             :         /**
    2214             :          * notify the source-side available actions
    2215             :          *
    2216             :          * This event indicates the actions offered by the data source.
    2217             :          * It will be sent immediately after creating the wl_data_offer
    2218             :          * object, or anytime the source side changes its offered actions
    2219             :          * through wl_data_source.set_actions.
    2220             :          * @param source_actions actions offered by the data source
    2221             :          * @since 3
    2222             :          */
    2223             :         void (*source_actions)(void *data,
    2224             :                                struct wl_data_offer *wl_data_offer,
    2225             :                                uint32_t source_actions);
    2226             :         /**
    2227             :          * notify the selected action
    2228             :          *
    2229             :          * This event indicates the action selected by the compositor
    2230             :          * after matching the source/destination side actions. Only one
    2231             :          * action (or none) will be offered here.
    2232             :          *
    2233             :          * This event can be emitted multiple times during the
    2234             :          * drag-and-drop operation in response to destination side action
    2235             :          * changes through wl_data_offer.set_actions.
    2236             :          *
    2237             :          * This event will no longer be emitted after wl_data_device.drop
    2238             :          * happened on the drag-and-drop destination, the client must honor
    2239             :          * the last action received, or the last preferred one set through
    2240             :          * wl_data_offer.set_actions when handling an "ask" action.
    2241             :          *
    2242             :          * Compositors may also change the selected action on the fly,
    2243             :          * mainly in response to keyboard modifier changes during the
    2244             :          * drag-and-drop operation.
    2245             :          *
    2246             :          * The most recent action received is always the valid one. Prior
    2247             :          * to receiving wl_data_device.drop, the chosen action may change
    2248             :          * (e.g. due to keyboard modifiers being pressed). At the time of
    2249             :          * receiving wl_data_device.drop the drag-and-drop destination must
    2250             :          * honor the last action received.
    2251             :          *
    2252             :          * Action changes may still happen after wl_data_device.drop,
    2253             :          * especially on "ask" actions, where the drag-and-drop destination
    2254             :          * may choose another action afterwards. Action changes happening
    2255             :          * at this stage are always the result of inter-client negotiation,
    2256             :          * the compositor shall no longer be able to induce a different
    2257             :          * action.
    2258             :          *
    2259             :          * Upon "ask" actions, it is expected that the drag-and-drop
    2260             :          * destination may potentially choose a different action and/or
    2261             :          * mime type, based on wl_data_offer.source_actions and finally
    2262             :          * chosen by the user (e.g. popping up a menu with the available
    2263             :          * options). The final wl_data_offer.set_actions and
    2264             :          * wl_data_offer.accept requests must happen before the call to
    2265             :          * wl_data_offer.finish.
    2266             :          * @param dnd_action action selected by the compositor
    2267             :          * @since 3
    2268             :          */
    2269             :         void (*action)(void *data,
    2270             :                        struct wl_data_offer *wl_data_offer,
    2271             :                        uint32_t dnd_action);
    2272             : };

    2345             : /**
    2346             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_data_offer
    2347             :  *
    2348             :  * Indicate that the client can accept the given mime type, or
    2349             :  * NULL for not accepted.
    2350             :  *
    2351             :  * For objects of version 2 or older, this request is used by the
    2352             :  * client to give feedback whether the client can receive the given
    2353             :  * mime type, or NULL if none is accepted; the feedback does not
    2354             :  * determine whether the drag-and-drop operation succeeds or not.
    2355             :  *
    2356             :  * For objects of version 3 or newer, this request determines the
    2357             :  * final result of the drag-and-drop operation. If the end result
    2358             :  * is that no mime types were accepted, the drag-and-drop operation
    2359             :  * will be cancelled and the corresponding drag source will receive
    2360             :  * wl_data_source.cancelled. Clients may still use this event in
    2361             :  * conjunction with wl_data_source.action for feedback.

    2370             : /**
    2371             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_data_offer
    2372             :  *
    2373             :  * To transfer the offered data, the client issues this request
    2374             :  * and indicates the mime type it wants to receive.  The transfer
    2375             :  * happens through the passed file descriptor (typically created
    2376             :  * with the pipe system call).  The source client writes the data
    2377             :  * in the mime type representation requested and then closes the
    2378             :  * file descriptor.
    2379             :  *
    2380             :  * The receiving client reads from the read end of the pipe until
    2381             :  * EOF and then closes its end, at which point the transfer is
    2382             :  * complete.
    2383             :  *
    2384             :  * This request may happen multiple times for different mime types,

    2386             :  * clients may preemptively fetch data or examine it more closely to
    2387             :  * determine acceptance.

    2433             : /**
    2434             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_data_offer
    2435             :  *
    2436             :  * Sets the actions that the destination side client supports for
    2437             :  * this operation. This request may trigger the emission of
    2438             :  * wl_data_source.action and wl_data_offer.action events if the compositor

    2443             :  * or wl_data_device.motion events.

    2447             :  * the drag source will receive wl_data_source.cancelled.

    2511             :          * it.

    2519             :         /**
    2520             :          * selection was cancelled
    2521             :          *
    2522             :          * This data source is no longer valid. There are several reasons
    2523             :          * why this could happen:
    2524             :          *
    2525             :          * - The data source has been replaced by another data source. -
    2526             :          * The drag-and-drop operation was performed, but the drop
    2527             :          * destination did not accept any of the mime types offered through
    2528             :          * wl_data_source.target. - The drag-and-drop operation was
    2529             :          * performed, but the drop destination did not select any of the
    2530             :          * actions present in the mask offered through
    2531             :          * wl_data_source.action. - The drag-and-drop operation was
    2532             :          * performed but didn't happen over a surface. - The compositor
    2533             :          * cancelled the drag-and-drop operation (e.g. compositor dependent
    2534             :          * timeouts to avoid stale drag-and-drop transfers).
    2535             :          *
    2536             :          * The client should clean up and destroy this data source.
    2537             :          *
    2538             :          * For objects of version 2 or older, wl_data_source.cancelled will
    2539             :          * only be emitted if the data source was replaced by another data
    2540             :          * source.
    2541             :          */
    2542             :         void (*cancelled)(void *data,
    2543             :                           struct wl_data_source *wl_data_source);
    2544             :         /**
    2545             :          * the drag-and-drop operation physically finished
    2546             :          *
    2547             :          * The user performed the drop action. This event does not
    2548             :          * indicate acceptance, wl_data_source.cancelled may still be
    2549             :          * emitted afterwards if the drop destination does not accept any
    2550             :          * mime type.
    2551             :          *
    2552             :          * However, this event might however not be received if the
    2553             :          * compositor cancelled the drag-and-drop operation before this
    2554             :          * event could happen.
    2555             :          *

    2557             :          * should not be destroyed here.
    2558             :          * @since 3
    2559             :          */
    2560             :         void (*dnd_drop_performed)(void *data,
    2561             :                                    struct wl_data_source *wl_data_source);
    2562             :         /**
    2563             :          * the drag-and-drop operation concluded
    2564             :          *
    2565             :          * The drop destination finished interoperating with this data
    2566             :          * source, so the client is now free to destroy this data source
    2567             :          * and free all associated data.

    2577             :          *
    2578             :          * This event indicates the action selected by the compositor
    2579             :          * after matching the source/destination side actions. Only one
    2580             :          * action (or none) will be offered here.
    2581             :          *
    2582             :          * This event can be emitted multiple times during the
    2583             :          * drag-and-drop operation, mainly in response to destination side
    2584             :          * changes through wl_data_offer.set_actions, and as the data
    2585             :          * device enters/leaves surfaces.
    2586             :          *
    2587             :          * It is only possible to receive this event after

    2589             :          * ended in an "ask" action, in which case the final
    2590             :          * wl_data_source.action event will happen immediately before
    2591             :          * wl_data_source.dnd_finished.
    2592             :          *
    2593             :          * Compositors may also change the selected action on the fly,
    2594             :          * mainly in response to keyboard modifier changes during the
    2595             :          * drag-and-drop operation.
    2596             :          *
    2597             :          * The most recent action received is always the valid one. The
    2598             :          * chosen action may change alongside negotiation (e.g. an "ask"
    2599             :          * action can turn into a "move" operation), so the effects of the
    2600             :          * final action must always be applied in

    2602             :          *
    2603             :          * Clients can trigger cursor surface changes from this point, so
    2604             :          * they reflect the current action.
    2605             :          * @param dnd_action action selected by the compositor
    2606             :          * @since 3
    2607             :          */
    2608             :         void (*action)(void *data,
    2609             :                        struct wl_data_source *wl_data_source,

    2756             :          * introduce a new wl_data_offer

    2758             :          * The data_offer event introduces a new wl_data_offer object,
    2759             :          * which will subsequently be used in either the data_device.enter
    2760             :          * event (for drag-and-drop) or the data_device.selection event
    2761             :          * (for selections). Immediately following the

    2768             :                            struct wl_data_device *wl_data_device,

    2770             :         /**
    2771             :          * initiate drag-and-drop session
    2772             :          *
    2773             :          * This event is sent when an active drag-and-drop pointer enters
    2774             :          * a surface owned by the client. The position of the pointer at
    2775             :          * enter time is provided by the x and y arguments, in
    2776             :          * surface-local coordinates.
    2777             :          * @param serial serial number of the enter event
    2778             :          * @param surface client surface entered
    2779             :          * @param x surface-local x coordinate
    2780             :          * @param y surface-local y coordinate
    2781             :          * @param id source data_offer object

    2784             :                       struct wl_data_device *wl_data_device,
    2785             :                       uint32_t serial,

    2787             :                       wl_fixed_t x,
    2788             :                       wl_fixed_t y,
    2789             :                       struct wl_data_offer *id);
    2790             :         /**
    2791             :          * end drag-and-drop session
    2792             :          *
    2793             :          * This event is sent when the drag-and-drop pointer leaves the
    2794             :          * surface and the session ends. The client must destroy the
    2795             :          * wl_data_offer introduced at enter time at this point.
    2796             :          */
    2797             :         void (*leave)(void *data,
    2798             :                       struct wl_data_device *wl_data_device);
    2799             :         /**

    2801             :          *
    2802             :          * This event is sent when the drag-and-drop pointer moves within
    2803             :          * the currently focused surface. The new position of the pointer
    2804             :          * is provided by the x and y arguments, in surface-local
    2805             :          * coordinates.
    2806             :          * @param time timestamp with millisecond granularity
    2807             :          * @param x surface-local x coordinate
    2808             :          * @param y surface-local y coordinate

    2815             :         /**
    2816             :          * end drag-and-drop session successfully
    2817             :          *
    2818             :          * The event is sent when a drag-and-drop operation is ended
    2819             :          * because the implicit grab is removed.
    2820             :          *
    2821             :          * The drag-and-drop destination is expected to honor the last
    2822             :          * action received through wl_data_offer.action, if the resulting
    2823             :          * action is "copy" or "move", the destination can still perform
    2824             :          * wl_data_offer.receive requests, and is expected to end all
    2825             :          * transfers with a wl_data_offer.finish request.
    2826             :          *
    2827             :          * If the resulting action is "ask", the action will not be
    2828             :          * considered final. The drag-and-drop destination is expected to
    2829             :          * perform one last wl_data_offer.set_actions request, or
    2830             :          * wl_data_offer.destroy in order to cancel the operation.
    2831             :          */

    2833             :                      struct wl_data_device *wl_data_device);
    2834             :         /**

    2837             :          * The selection event is sent out to notify the client of a new
    2838             :          * wl_data_offer for the selection for this device. The
    2839             :          * data_device.data_offer and the data_offer.offer events are sent
    2840             :          * out immediately before this event to introduce the data offer
    2841             :          * object. The selection event is sent to a client immediately
    2842             :          * before receiving keyboard focus and when a new selection is set

    2844             :          * until a new data_offer or NULL is received or until the client

    2846             :          * within the same client doesn't mean a new selection will be
    2847             :          * sent. The client must destroy the previous selection data_offer,

    2936             : /**
    2937             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_data_device
    2938             :  *
    2939             :  * This request asks the compositor to start a drag-and-drop
    2940             :  * operation on behalf of the client.
    2941             :  *
    2942             :  * The source argument is the data source that provides the data
    2943             :  * for the eventual data transfer. If source is NULL, enter, leave
    2944             :  * and motion events are sent only to the client that initiated the
    2945             :  * drag and the client is expected to handle the data passing
    2946             :  * internally. If source is destroyed, the drag-and-drop session will be
    2947             :  * cancelled.
    2948             :  *
    2949             :  * The origin surface is the surface where the drag originates and
    2950             :  * the client must have an active implicit grab that matches the
    2951             :  * serial.
    2952             :  *
    2953             :  * The icon surface is an optional (can be NULL) surface that
    2954             :  * provides an icon to be moved around with the cursor.  Initially,
    2955             :  * the top-left corner of the icon surface is placed at the cursor
    2956             :  * hotspot, but subsequent wl_surface.offset requests can move the
    2957             :  * relative position. Attach requests must be confirmed with
    2958             :  * wl_surface.commit as usual. The icon surface is given the role of
    2959             :  * a drag-and-drop icon. If the icon surface already has another role,
    2960             :  * it raises a protocol error.
    2961             :  *
    2962             :  * The input region is ignored for wl_surfaces with the role of a
    2963             :  * drag-and-drop icon.
    2964             :  *
    2965             :  * The given source may not be used in any further set_selection or
    2966             :  * start_drag requests. Attempting to reuse a previously-used source
    2967             :  * may send a used_source error.
    2968             :  */

    3007             : #ifndef WL_DATA_DEVICE_MANAGER_DND_ACTION_ENUM
    3008             : #define WL_DATA_DEVICE_MANAGER_DND_ACTION_ENUM
    3009             : /**
    3010             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_data_device_manager
    3011             :  * drag and drop actions
    3012             :  *
    3013             :  * This is a bitmask of the available/preferred actions in a
    3014             :  * drag-and-drop operation.
    3015             :  *
    3016             :  * In the compositor, the selected action is a result of matching the
    3017             :  * actions offered by the source and destination sides.  "action" events
    3018             :  * with a "none" action will be sent to both source and destination if
    3019             :  * there is no match. All further checks will effectively happen on
    3020             :  * (source actions ∩ destination actions).
    3021             :  *
    3022             :  * In addition, compositors may also pick different actions in
    3023             :  * reaction to key modifiers being pressed. One common design that
    3024             :  * is used in major toolkits (and the behavior recommended for
    3025             :  * compositors) is:
    3026             :  *
    3027             :  * - If no modifiers are pressed, the first match (in bit order)
    3028             :  * will be used.
    3029             :  * - Pressing Shift selects "move", if enabled in the mask.
    3030             :  * - Pressing Control selects "copy", if enabled in the mask.
    3031             :  *
    3032             :  * Behavior beyond that is considered implementation-dependent.
    3033             :  * Compositors may for example bind other modifiers (like Alt/Meta)
    3034             :  * or drags initiated with other buttons than BTN_LEFT to specific
    3035             :  * actions (e.g. "ask").
    3036             :  */

    3296             : struct wl_shell_surface_listener {
    3297             :         /**
    3298             :          * ping client
    3299             :          *
    3300             :          * Ping a client to check if it is receiving events and sending
    3301             :          * requests. A client is expected to reply with a pong request.
    3302             :          * @param serial serial number of the ping
    3303             :          */
    3304             :         void (*ping)(void *data,
    3305             :                      struct wl_shell_surface *wl_shell_surface,
    3306             :                      uint32_t serial);
    3307             :         /**
    3308             :          * suggest resize
    3309             :          *
    3310             :          * The configure event asks the client to resize its surface.
    3311             :          *
    3312             :          * The size is a hint, in the sense that the client is free to
    3313             :          * ignore it if it doesn't resize, pick a smaller size (to satisfy
    3314             :          * aspect ratio or resize in steps of NxM pixels).
    3315             :          *
    3316             :          * The edges parameter provides a hint about how the surface was
    3317             :          * resized. The client may use this information to decide how to
    3318             :          * adjust its content to the new size (e.g. a scrolling area might
    3319             :          * adjust its content position to leave the viewable content
    3320             :          * unmoved).
    3321             :          *
    3322             :          * The client is free to dismiss all but the last configure event
    3323             :          * it received.
    3324             :          *

    3527             : /**
    3528             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_shell_surface
    3529             :  *
    3530             :  * Map the surface as a fullscreen surface.
    3531             :  *
    3532             :  * If an output parameter is given then the surface will be made
    3533             :  * fullscreen on that output. If the client does not specify the
    3534             :  * output then the compositor will apply its policy - usually
    3535             :  * choosing the output on which the surface has the biggest surface
    3536             :  * area.
    3537             :  *
    3538             :  * The client may specify a method to resolve a size conflict
    3539             :  * between the output size and the surface size - this is provided
    3540             :  * through the method parameter.
    3541             :  *
    3542             :  * The framerate parameter is used only when the method is set
    3543             :  * to "driver", to indicate the preferred framerate. A value of 0
    3544             :  * indicates that the client does not care about framerate.  The
    3545             :  * framerate is specified in mHz, that is framerate of 60000 is 60Hz.
    3546             :  *
    3547             :  * A method of "scale" or "driver" implies a scaling operation of
    3548             :  * the surface, either via a direct scaling operation or a change of
    3549             :  * the output mode. This will override any kind of output scaling, so
    3550             :  * that mapping a surface with a buffer size equal to the mode can
    3551             :  * fill the screen independent of buffer_scale.
    3552             :  *
    3553             :  * A method of "fill" means we don't scale up the buffer, however
    3554             :  * any output scale is applied. This means that you may run into
    3555             :  * an edge case where the application maps a buffer with the same
    3556             :  * size of the output mode but buffer_scale 1 (thus making a
    3557             :  * surface larger than the output). In this case it is allowed to
    3558             :  * downscale the results to fit the screen.
    3559             :  *
    3560             :  * The compositor must reply to this request with a configure event
    3561             :  * with the dimensions for the output on which the surface will
    3562             :  * be made fullscreen.
    3563             :  */
    3564             : static inline void
    3565             : wl_shell_surface_set_fullscreen(struct wl_shell_surface *wl_shell_surface, uint32_t method, uint32_t framerate, struct wl_output *output)
    3566             : {
    3567             :         wl_proxy_marshal_flags((struct wl_proxy *) wl_shell_surface,
    3568             :                          WL_SHELL_SURFACE_SET_FULLSCREEN, NULL, wl_proxy_get_version((struct wl_proxy *) wl_shell_surface), 0, method, framerate, output);
    3569             : }
    3570             : 
    3571             : /**
    3572             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_shell_surface
    3573             :  *
    3574             :  * Map the surface as a popup.
    3575             :  *
    3576             :  * A popup surface is a transient surface with an added pointer
    3577             :  * grab.
    3578             :  *
    3579             :  * An existing implicit grab will be changed to owner-events mode,
    3580             :  * and the popup grab will continue after the implicit grab ends
    3581             :  * (i.e. releasing the mouse button does not cause the popup to
    3582             :  * be unmapped).
    3583             :  *
    3584             :  * The popup grab continues until the window is destroyed or a
    3585             :  * mouse button is pressed in any other client's window. A click
    3586             :  * in any of the client's surfaces is reported as normal, however,
    3587             :  * clicks in other clients' surfaces will be discarded and trigger
    3588             :  * the callback.
    3589             :  *
    3590             :  * The x and y arguments specify the location of the upper left
    3591             :  * corner of the surface relative to the upper left corner of the
    3592             :  * parent surface, in surface-local coordinates.
    3593             :  */
    3594             : static inline void
    3595             : wl_shell_surface_set_popup(struct wl_shell_surface *wl_shell_surface, struct wl_seat *seat, uint32_t serial, struct wl_surface *parent, int32_t x, int32_t y, uint32_t flags)
    3596             : {

    3598             :                          WL_SHELL_SURFACE_SET_POPUP, NULL, wl_proxy_get_version((struct wl_proxy *) wl_shell_surface), 0, seat, serial, parent, x, y, flags);
    3599             : }

    3606             :  * If an output parameter is given then the surface will be

    3618             :  * the main difference between a maximized shell surface and a
    3619             :  * fullscreen shell surface.

    3701             : struct wl_surface_listener {
    3702             :         /**
    3703             :          * surface enters an output
    3704             :          *
    3705             :          * This is emitted whenever a surface's creation, movement, or
    3706             :          * resizing results in some part of it being within the scanout
    3707             :          * region of an output.
    3708             :          *
    3709             :          * Note that a surface may be overlapping with zero or more
    3710             :          * outputs.
    3711             :          * @param output output entered by the surface
    3712             :          */
    3713             :         void (*enter)(void *data,
    3714             :                       struct wl_surface *wl_surface,
    3715             :                       struct wl_output *output);
    3716             :         /**
    3717             :          * surface leaves an output
    3718             :          *
    3719             :          * This is emitted whenever a surface's creation, movement, or
    3720             :          * resizing results in it no longer having any part of it within
    3721             :          * the scanout region of an output.
    3722             :          *
    3723             :          * Clients should not use the number of outputs the surface is on
    3724             :          * for frame throttling purposes. The surface might be hidden even
    3725             :          * if no leave event has been sent, and the compositor might expect
    3726             :          * new surface content updates even if no enter event has been
    3727             :          * sent. The frame event should be used instead.
    3728             :          * @param output output left by the surface
    3729             :          */
    3730             :         void (*leave)(void *data,
    3731             :                       struct wl_surface *wl_surface,
    3732             :                       struct wl_output *output);
    3733             :         /**
    3734             :          * preferred buffer scale for the surface
    3735             :          *
    3736             :          * This event indicates the preferred buffer scale for this
    3737             :          * surface. It is sent whenever the compositor's preference
    3738             :          * changes.
    3739             :          *
    3740             :          * Before receiving this event the preferred buffer scale for this
    3741             :          * surface is 1.
    3742             :          *
    3743             :          * It is intended that scaling aware clients use this event to
    3744             :          * scale their content and use wl_surface.set_buffer_scale to
    3745             :          * indicate the scale they have rendered with. This allows clients
    3746             :          * to supply a higher detail buffer.
    3747             :          *
    3748             :          * The compositor shall emit a scale value greater than 0.
    3749             :          * @param factor preferred scaling factor
    3750             :          * @since 6
    3751             :          */
    3752             :         void (*preferred_buffer_scale)(void *data,
    3753             :                                        struct wl_surface *wl_surface,
    3754             :                                        int32_t factor);
    3755             :         /**
    3756             :          * preferred buffer transform for the surface
    3757             :          *
    3758             :          * This event indicates the preferred buffer transform for this
    3759             :          * surface. It is sent whenever the compositor's preference
    3760             :          * changes.
    3761             :          *
    3762             :          * Before receiving this event the preferred buffer transform for
    3763             :          * this surface is normal.
    3764             :          *
    3765             :          * Applying this transformation to the surface buffer contents and
    3766             :          * using wl_surface.set_buffer_transform might allow the compositor
    3767             :          * to use the surface buffer more efficiently.
    3768             :          * @param transform preferred transform
    3769             :          * @since 6
    3770             :          */
    3771             :         void (*preferred_buffer_transform)(void *data,
    3772             :                                            struct wl_surface *wl_surface,
    3773             :                                            uint32_t transform);
    3774             : };

    3892             : 
    3893             : /**
    3894             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_surface
    3895             :  *
    3896             :  * Set a buffer as the content of this surface.
    3897             :  *
    3898             :  * The new size of the surface is calculated based on the buffer
    3899             :  * size transformed by the inverse buffer_transform and the
    3900             :  * inverse buffer_scale. This means that at commit time the supplied
    3901             :  * buffer size must be an integer multiple of the buffer_scale. If
    3902             :  * that's not the case, an invalid_size error is sent.
    3903             :  *
    3904             :  * The x and y arguments specify the location of the new pending
    3905             :  * buffer's upper left corner, relative to the current buffer's upper
    3906             :  * left corner, in surface-local coordinates. In other words, the
    3907             :  * x and y, combined with the new surface size define in which
    3908             :  * directions the surface's size changes. Setting anything other than 0
    3909             :  * as x and y arguments is discouraged, and should instead be replaced
    3910             :  * with using the separate wl_surface.offset request.
    3911             :  *
    3912             :  * When the bound wl_surface version is 5 or higher, passing any
    3913             :  * non-zero x or y is a protocol violation, and will result in an
    3914             :  * 'invalid_offset' error being raised. The x and y arguments are ignored
    3915             :  * and do not change the pending state. To achieve equivalent semantics,
    3916             :  * use wl_surface.offset.
    3917             :  *
    3918             :  * Surface contents are double-buffered state, see wl_surface.commit.
    3919             :  *
    3920             :  * The initial surface contents are void; there is no content.
    3921             :  * wl_surface.attach assigns the given wl_buffer as the pending
    3922             :  * wl_buffer. wl_surface.commit makes the pending wl_buffer the new
    3923             :  * surface contents, and the size of the surface becomes the size
    3924             :  * calculated from the wl_buffer, as described above. After commit,
    3925             :  * there is no pending buffer until the next attach.
    3926             :  *
    3927             :  * Committing a pending wl_buffer allows the compositor to read the
    3928             :  * pixels in the wl_buffer. The compositor may access the pixels at
    3929             :  * any time after the wl_surface.commit request. When the compositor
    3930             :  * will not access the pixels anymore, it will send the
    3931             :  * wl_buffer.release event. Only after receiving wl_buffer.release,
    3932             :  * the client may reuse the wl_buffer. A wl_buffer that has been
    3933             :  * attached and then replaced by another attach instead of committed
    3934             :  * will not receive a release event, and is not used by the
    3935             :  * compositor.
    3936             :  *
    3937             :  * If a pending wl_buffer has been committed to more than one wl_surface,
    3938             :  * the delivery of wl_buffer.release events becomes undefined. A well
    3939             :  * behaved client should not rely on wl_buffer.release events in this
    3940             :  * case. Alternatively, a client could create multiple wl_buffer objects
    3941             :  * from the same backing storage or use a protocol extension providing
    3942             :  * per-commit release notifications.
    3943             :  *
    3944             :  * Destroying the wl_buffer after wl_buffer.release does not change
    3945             :  * the surface contents. Destroying the wl_buffer before wl_buffer.release
    3946             :  * is allowed as long as the underlying buffer storage isn't re-used (this
    3947             :  * can happen e.g. on client process termination). However, if the client
    3948             :  * destroys the wl_buffer before receiving the wl_buffer.release event and
    3949             :  * mutates the underlying buffer storage, the surface contents become
    3950             :  * undefined immediately.
    3951             :  *
    3952             :  * If wl_surface.attach is sent with a NULL wl_buffer, the
    3953             :  * following wl_surface.commit will remove the surface content.
    3954             :  *
    3955             :  * If a pending wl_buffer has been destroyed, the result is not specified.
    3956             :  * Many compositors are known to remove the surface content on the following
    3957             :  * wl_surface.commit, but this behaviour is not universal. Clients seeking to
    3958             :  * maximise compatibility should not destroy pending buffers and should
    3959             :  * ensure that they explicitly remove content from surfaces, even after
    3960             :  * destroying buffers.
    3961             :  */
    3962             : static inline void
    3963           0 : wl_surface_attach(struct wl_surface *wl_surface, struct wl_buffer *buffer, int32_t x, int32_t y)
    3964             : {
    3965           0 :         wl_proxy_marshal_flags((struct wl_proxy *) wl_surface,
    3966             :                          WL_SURFACE_ATTACH, NULL, wl_proxy_get_version((struct wl_proxy *) wl_surface), 0, buffer, x, y);
    3967           0 : }
    3968             : 
    3969             : /**

    4001             : /**
    4002             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_surface
    4003             :  *
    4004             :  * Request a notification when it is a good time to start drawing a new
    4005             :  * frame, by creating a frame callback. This is useful for throttling
    4006             :  * redrawing operations, and driving animations.
    4007             :  *
    4008             :  * When a client is animating on a wl_surface, it can use the 'frame'
    4009             :  * request to get notified when it is a good time to draw and commit the
    4010             :  * next frame of animation. If the client commits an update earlier than
    4011             :  * that, it is likely that some updates will not make it to the display,
    4012             :  * and the client is wasting resources by drawing too often.
    4013             :  *
    4014             :  * The frame request will take effect on the next wl_surface.commit.
    4015             :  * The notification will only be posted for one frame unless
    4016             :  * requested again. For a wl_surface, the notifications are posted in
    4017             :  * the order the frame requests were committed.
    4018             :  *
    4019             :  * The server must send the notifications so that a client
    4020             :  * will not send excessive updates, while still allowing
    4021             :  * the highest possible update rate for clients that wait for the reply
    4022             :  * before drawing again. The server should give some time for the client
    4023             :  * to draw and commit after sending the frame callback events to let it
    4024             :  * hit the next output refresh.
    4025             :  *
    4026             :  * A server should avoid signaling the frame callbacks if the
    4027             :  * surface is not visible in any way, e.g. the surface is off-screen,
    4028             :  * or completely obscured by other opaque surfaces.
    4029             :  *
    4030             :  * The object returned by this request will be destroyed by the
    4031             :  * compositor after the callback is fired and as such the client must not
    4032             :  * attempt to use it after that point.
    4033             :  *
    4034             :  * The callback_data passed in the callback is the current time, in
    4035             :  * milliseconds, with an undefined base.
    4036             :  */
    4037             : static inline struct wl_callback *
    4038           0 : wl_surface_frame(struct wl_surface *wl_surface)
    4039             : {
    4040             :         struct wl_proxy *callback;
    4041             : 
    4042           0 :         callback = wl_proxy_marshal_flags((struct wl_proxy *) wl_surface,
    4043             :                          WL_SURFACE_FRAME, &wl_callback_interface, wl_proxy_get_version((struct wl_proxy *) wl_surface), 0, NULL);
    4044             : 
    4045           0 :         return (struct wl_callback *) callback;
    4046             : }

    4048             : /**

    4066             :  *

    4068             :  * wl_surface.commit copies the pending region to the current region.
    4069             :  * Otherwise, the pending and current regions are never changed.

    4071             :  * The initial value for an opaque region is empty. Setting the pending

    4116             : /**
    4117             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_surface
    4118             :  *
    4119             :  * Surface state (input, opaque, and damage regions, attached buffers,
    4120             :  * etc.) is double-buffered. Protocol requests modify the pending state,
    4121             :  * as opposed to the active state in use by the compositor.
    4122             :  *
    4123             :  * A commit request atomically creates a content update from the pending
    4124             :  * state, even if the pending state has not been touched. The content
    4125             :  * update is placed in a queue until it becomes active. After commit, the
    4126             :  * new pending state is as documented for each related request.
    4127             :  *
    4128             :  * When the content update is applied, the wl_buffer is applied before all
    4129             :  * other state. This means that all coordinates in double-buffered state
    4130             :  * are relative to the newly attached wl_buffers, except for
    4131             :  * wl_surface.attach itself. If there is no newly attached wl_buffer, the
    4132             :  * coordinates are relative to the previous content update.
    4133             :  *
    4134             :  * All requests that need a commit to become effective are documented
    4135             :  * to affect double-buffered state.
    4136             :  *
    4137             :  * Other interfaces may add further double-buffered surface state.
    4138             :  */
    4139             : static inline void
    4140           0 : wl_surface_commit(struct wl_surface *wl_surface)
    4141             : {
    4142           0 :         wl_proxy_marshal_flags((struct wl_proxy *) wl_surface,
    4143             :                          WL_SURFACE_COMMIT, NULL, wl_proxy_get_version((struct wl_proxy *) wl_surface), 0);
