    4146             : /**
    4147             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_surface
    4148             :  *
    4149             :  * This request sets the transformation that the client has already applied
    4150             :  * to the content of the buffer. The accepted values for the transform
    4151             :  * parameter are the values for wl_output.transform.
    4152             :  *
    4153             :  * The compositor applies the inverse of this transformation whenever it
    4154             :  * uses the buffer contents.
    4155             :  *
    4156             :  * Buffer transform is double-buffered state, see wl_surface.commit.
    4157             :  *
    4158             :  * A newly created surface has its buffer transformation set to normal.
    4159             :  *
    4160             :  * wl_surface.set_buffer_transform changes the pending buffer
    4161             :  * transformation. wl_surface.commit copies the pending buffer
    4162             :  * transformation to the current one. Otherwise, the pending and current
    4163             :  * values are never changed.
    4164             :  *
    4165             :  * The purpose of this request is to allow clients to render content
    4166             :  * according to the output transform, thus permitting the compositor to
    4167             :  * use certain optimizations even if the display is rotated. Using
    4168             :  * hardware overlays and scanning out a client buffer for fullscreen
    4169             :  * surfaces are examples of such optimizations. Those optimizations are
    4170             :  * highly dependent on the compositor implementation, so the use of this
    4171             :  * request should be considered on a case-by-case basis.
    4172             :  *
    4173             :  * Note that if the transform value includes 90 or 270 degree rotation,
    4174             :  * the width of the buffer will become the surface height and the height
    4175             :  * of the buffer will become the surface width.
    4176             :  *
    4177             :  * If transform is not one of the values from the
    4178             :  * wl_output.transform enum the invalid_transform protocol error
    4179             :  * is raised.
    4180             :  */
    4181             : static inline void

    4230             :  * Damage is double-buffered state, see wl_surface.commit.
    4231             :  *
    4232             :  * The damage rectangle is specified in buffer coordinates,
    4233             :  * where x and y specify the upper left corner of the damage rectangle.
    4234             :  *
    4235             :  * The initial value for pending damage is empty: no damage.
    4236             :  * wl_surface.damage_buffer adds pending damage: the new pending
    4237             :  * damage is the union of old pending damage and the given rectangle.
    4238             :  *
    4239             :  * wl_surface.commit assigns pending damage as the current damage,
    4240             :  * and clears pending damage. The server will clear the current
    4241             :  * damage as it repaints the surface.
    4242             :  *
    4243             :  * This request differs from wl_surface.damage in only one way - it
    4244             :  * takes damage in buffer coordinates instead of surface-local
    4245             :  * coordinates. While this generally is more intuitive than surface
    4246             :  * coordinates, it is especially desirable when using wp_viewport
    4247             :  * or when a drawing library (like EGL) is unaware of buffer scale
    4248             :  * and buffer transform.
    4249             :  *
    4250             :  * Note: Because buffer transformation changes and damage requests may
    4251             :  * be interleaved in the protocol stream, it is impossible to determine
    4252             :  * the actual mapping between surface and buffer damage until
    4253             :  * wl_surface.commit time. Therefore, compositors wishing to take both
    4254             :  * kinds of damage into account will have to accumulate damage from the
    4255             :  * two requests separately and only transform from one to the other
    4256             :  * after receiving the wl_surface.commit.
    4257             :  */

    4336             : struct wl_seat_listener {
    4337             :         /**
    4338             :          * seat capabilities changed
    4339             :          *
    4340             :          * This is sent on binding to the seat global or whenever a seat
    4341             :          * gains or loses the pointer, keyboard or touch capabilities. The
    4342             :          * argument is a capability enum containing the complete set of
    4343             :          * capabilities this seat has.
    4344             :          *
    4345             :          * When the pointer capability is added, a client may create a
    4346             :          * wl_pointer object using the wl_seat.get_pointer request. This
    4347             :          * object will receive pointer events until the capability is
    4348             :          * removed in the future.
    4349             :          *
    4350             :          * When the pointer capability is removed, a client should destroy
    4351             :          * the wl_pointer objects associated with the seat where the
    4352             :          * capability was removed, using the wl_pointer.release request. No
    4353             :          * further pointer events will be received on these objects.
    4354             :          *
    4355             :          * In some compositors, if a seat regains the pointer capability
    4356             :          * and a client has a previously obtained wl_pointer object of
    4357             :          * version 4 or less, that object may start sending pointer events
    4358             :          * again. This behavior is considered a misinterpretation of the
    4359             :          * intended behavior and must not be relied upon by the client.
    4360             :          * wl_pointer objects of version 5 or later must not send events if
    4361             :          * created before the most recent event notifying the client of an
    4362             :          * added pointer capability.
    4363             :          *
    4364             :          * The above behavior also applies to wl_keyboard and wl_touch with
    4365             :          * the keyboard and touch capabilities, respectively.
    4366             :          * @param capabilities capabilities of the seat
    4367             :          */
    4368             :         void (*capabilities)(void *data,
    4369             :                              struct wl_seat *wl_seat,
    4370             :                              uint32_t capabilities);
    4371             :         /**
    4372             :          * unique identifier for this seat
    4373             :          *
    4374             :          * In a multi-seat configuration the seat name can be used by
    4375             :          * clients to help identify which physical devices the seat
    4376             :          * represents.
    4377             :          *
    4378             :          * The seat name is a UTF-8 string with no convention defined for
    4379             :          * its contents. Each name is unique among all wl_seat globals. The
    4380             :          * name is only guaranteed to be unique for the current compositor
    4381             :          * instance.
    4382             :          *
    4383             :          * The same seat names are used for all clients. Thus, the name can
    4384             :          * be shared across processes to refer to a specific wl_seat
    4385             :          * global.
    4386             :          *
    4387             :          * The name event is sent after binding to the seat global, and
    4388             :          * should be sent before announcing capabilities. This event only
    4389             :          * sent once per seat object, and the name does not change over the
    4390             :          * lifetime of the wl_seat global.
    4391             :          *
    4392             :          * Compositors may re-use the same seat name if the wl_seat global
    4393             :          * is destroyed and re-created later.
    4394             :          * @param name seat identifier
    4395             :          * @since 2
    4396             :          */
    4397             :         void (*name)(void *data,
    4398             :                      struct wl_seat *wl_seat,
    4399             :                      const char *name);
    4400             : };

    4611             :  * client how an axis event was physically generated; a client may
    4612             :  * adjust the user interface accordingly. For example, scroll events
    4613             :  * from a "finger" source may be in a smooth coordinate space with
    4614             :  * kinetic scrolling whereas a "wheel" source may be in discrete steps
    4615             :  * of a number of lines.

    4618             :  * continuous coordinate space, but using something other than a
    4619             :  * finger. One example for this source is button-based scrolling where
    4620             :  * the vertical motion of a device is converted to scroll events while
    4621             :  * a button is held down.
    4622             :  *
    4623             :  * The "wheel tilt" axis source indicates that the actual device is a
    4624             :  * wheel but the scroll event is not caused by a rotation but a
    4625             :  * (usually sideways) tilt of the wheel.

    4761             :          * value parameter is the length of a vector along the specified
    4762             :          * axis in a coordinate space identical to those of motion events,
    4763             :          * representing a relative movement along the specified axis.
    4764             :          *

    4766             :          * axis events will be emitted.

    4781             :                      uint32_t axis,
    4782             :                      wl_fixed_t value);
    4783             :         /**
    4784             :          * end of a pointer event sequence
    4785             :          *
    4786             :          * Indicates the end of a set of events that logically belong
    4787             :          * together. A client is expected to accumulate the data in all
    4788             :          * events within the frame before proceeding.
    4789             :          *
    4790             :          * All wl_pointer events before a wl_pointer.frame event belong
    4791             :          * logically together. For example, in a diagonal scroll motion the
    4792             :          * compositor will send an optional wl_pointer.axis_source event,
    4793             :          * two wl_pointer.axis events (horizontal and vertical) and finally
    4794             :          * a wl_pointer.frame event. The client may use this information to
    4795             :          * calculate a diagonal vector for scrolling.
    4796             :          *
    4797             :          * When multiple wl_pointer.axis events occur within the same
    4798             :          * frame, the motion vector is the combined motion of all events.
    4799             :          * When a wl_pointer.axis and a wl_pointer.axis_stop event occur
    4800             :          * within the same frame, this indicates that axis movement in one
    4801             :          * axis has stopped but continues in the other axis. When multiple
    4802             :          * wl_pointer.axis_stop events occur within the same frame, this
    4803             :          * indicates that these axes stopped in the same instance.
    4804             :          *
    4805             :          * A wl_pointer.frame event is sent for every logical event group,
    4806             :          * even if the group only contains a single wl_pointer event.
    4807             :          * Specifically, a client may get a sequence: motion, frame,
    4808             :          * button, frame, axis, frame, axis_stop, frame.
    4809             :          *
    4810             :          * The wl_pointer.enter and wl_pointer.leave events are logical
    4811             :          * events generated by the compositor and not the hardware. These
    4812             :          * events are also grouped by a wl_pointer.frame. When a pointer
    4813             :          * moves from one surface to another, a compositor should group the
    4814             :          * wl_pointer.leave event within the same wl_pointer.frame.
    4815             :          * However, a client must not rely on wl_pointer.leave and
    4816             :          * wl_pointer.enter being in the same wl_pointer.frame.
    4817             :          * Compositor-specific policies may require the wl_pointer.leave
    4818             :          * and wl_pointer.enter event being split across multiple
    4819             :          * wl_pointer.frame groups.
    4820             :          * @since 5
    4821             :          */
    4822             :         void (*frame)(void *data,
    4823             :                       struct wl_pointer *wl_pointer);
    4824             :         /**
    4825             :          * axis source event
    4826             :          *
    4827             :          * Source information for scroll and other axes.
    4828             :          *
    4829             :          * This event does not occur on its own. It is sent before a
    4830             :          * wl_pointer.frame event and carries the source information for
    4831             :          * all events within that frame.
    4832             :          *
    4833             :          * The source specifies how this event was generated. If the source
    4834             :          * is wl_pointer.axis_source.finger, a wl_pointer.axis_stop event
    4835             :          * will be sent when the user lifts the finger off the device.
    4836             :          *
    4837             :          * If the source is wl_pointer.axis_source.wheel,
    4838             :          * wl_pointer.axis_source.wheel_tilt or
    4839             :          * wl_pointer.axis_source.continuous, a wl_pointer.axis_stop event
    4840             :          * may or may not be sent. Whether a compositor sends an axis_stop
    4841             :          * event for these sources is hardware-specific and
    4842             :          * implementation-dependent; clients must not rely on receiving an
    4843             :          * axis_stop event for these scroll sources and should treat scroll
    4844             :          * sequences from these scroll sources as unterminated by default.
    4845             :          *
    4846             :          * This event is optional. If the source is unknown for a
    4847             :          * particular axis event sequence, no event is sent. Only one
    4848             :          * wl_pointer.axis_source event is permitted per frame.
    4849             :          *
    4850             :          * The order of wl_pointer.axis_discrete and wl_pointer.axis_source
    4851             :          * is not guaranteed.

    4864             :          * event is sent to notify a client that the axis sequence has
    4865             :          * terminated. This enables the client to implement kinetic
    4866             :          * scrolling. See the wl_pointer.axis_source documentation for

    4883             :         /**
    4884             :          * axis click event
    4885             :          *
    4886             :          * Discrete step information for scroll and other axes.
    4887             :          *
    4888             :          * This event carries the axis value of the wl_pointer.axis event
    4889             :          * in discrete steps (e.g. mouse wheel clicks).
    4890             :          *
    4891             :          * This event is deprecated with wl_pointer version 8 - this event
    4892             :          * is not sent to clients supporting version 8 or later.
    4893             :          *
    4894             :          * This event does not occur on its own, it is coupled with a

    4896             :          * continuous scale. The protocol guarantees that each
    4897             :          * axis_discrete event is always followed by exactly one axis event
    4898             :          * with the same axis number within the same wl_pointer.frame. Note
    4899             :          * that the protocol allows for other events to occur between the

    4901             :          * axis_discrete or axis events. A wl_pointer.frame must not
    4902             :          * contain more than one axis_discrete event per axis type.
    4903             :          *

    4905             :          * two-finger scrolling on touchpads do not have discrete steps and
    4906             :          * do not generate this event.

    4909             :          * value of -2 is two steps towards the negative direction of this

    4995             :          * guaranteed.

    5103             :  *
    5104             :  * Set the pointer surface, i.e., the surface that contains the
    5105             :  * pointer image (cursor). This request gives the surface the role
    5106             :  * of a cursor. If the surface already has another role, it raises
    5107             :  * a protocol error.

    5109             :  * The cursor actually changes only if the pointer
    5110             :  * focus for this device is one of the requesting client's surfaces

    5112             :  * there was a previous surface set with this request it is

    5116             :  * the pointer surface relative to the pointer location. Its
    5117             :  * top-left corner is always at (x, y) - (hotspot_x, hotspot_y),
    5118             :  * where (x, y) are the coordinates of the pointer location, in
    5119             :  * surface-local coordinates.

    5122             :  * and hotspot_y are decremented by the x and y parameters
    5123             :  * passed to the request. The offset must be applied by
    5124             :  * wl_surface.commit as usual.

    5270             :          *

    5273             :          *

    5288             :         /**
    5289             :          * key event
    5290             :          *
    5291             :          * A key was pressed or released. The time argument is a
    5292             :          * timestamp with millisecond granularity, with an undefined base.
    5293             :          *
    5294             :          * The key is a platform-specific key code that can be interpreted
    5295             :          * by feeding it to the keyboard mapping (see the keymap event).
    5296             :          *

    5298             :          * wl_keyboard.modifiers event must be sent after this event.
    5299             :          *

    5301             :          * keys currently logically down (if the state argument is pressed)
    5302             :          * or removes the key from the keys currently logically down (if
    5303             :          * the state argument is released). The compositor must not send
    5304             :          * this event if the wl_keyboard did not have an active surface
    5305             :          * immediately before this event. The compositor must not send this
    5306             :          * event if state is pressed (resp. released) and the key was
    5307             :          * already logically down (resp. was not logically down)
    5308             :          * immediately before this event.
    5309             :          *
    5310             :          * Since version 10, compositors may send key events with the
    5311             :          * "repeated" key state when a wl_keyboard.repeat_info event with a
    5312             :          * rate argument of 0 has been received. This allows the compositor
    5313             :          * to take over the responsibility of key repetition.
    5314             :          * @param serial serial number of the key event

    5330             :          *
    5331             :          * The compositor may send this event without a surface of the
    5332             :          * client having keyboard focus, for example to tie modifier
    5333             :          * information to pointer focus instead. If a modifier event with

    5336             :          * the next wl_keyboard.modifiers event. In order to reset the

    5465             : struct wl_touch_listener {
    5466             :         /**
    5467             :          * touch down event and beginning of a touch sequence
    5468             :          *
    5469             :          * A new touch point has appeared on the surface. This touch
    5470             :          * point is assigned a unique ID. Future events from this touch
    5471             :          * point reference this ID. The ID ceases to be valid after a touch
    5472             :          * up event and may be reused in the future.

    5474             :          * @param time timestamp with millisecond granularity

    5478             :          * @param y surface-local y coordinate
    5479             :          */

    5481             :                      struct wl_touch *wl_touch,

    5483             :                      uint32_t time,
    5484             :                      struct wl_surface *surface,
    5485             :                      int32_t id,
    5486             :                      wl_fixed_t x,
    5487             :                      wl_fixed_t y);
    5488             :         /**
    5489             :          * end of a touch event sequence
    5490             :          *
    5491             :          * The touch point has disappeared. No further events will be
    5492             :          * sent for this touch point and the touch point's ID is released
    5493             :          * and may be reused in a future touch down event.
    5494             :          * @param serial serial number of the touch up event

    5496             :          * @param id the unique ID of this touch point
    5497             :          */

    5506             :          * A touch point has changed coordinates.

    5508             :          * @param id the unique ID of this touch point
    5509             :          * @param x surface-local x coordinate
    5510             :          * @param y surface-local y coordinate
    5511             :          */

    5518             :         /**
    5519             :          * end of touch frame event
    5520             :          *
    5521             :          * Indicates the end of a set of events that logically belong
    5522             :          * together. A client is expected to accumulate the data in all
    5523             :          * events within the frame before proceeding.
    5524             :          *
    5525             :          * A wl_touch.frame terminates at least one event but otherwise no
    5526             :          * guarantee is provided about the set of events within a frame. A
    5527             :          * client must assume that any state not updated in a frame is
    5528             :          * unchanged from the previously known state.
    5529             :          */
    5530             :         void (*frame)(void *data,
    5531             :                       struct wl_touch *wl_touch);
    5532             :         /**
    5533             :          * touch session cancelled
    5534             :          *
    5535             :          * Sent if the compositor decides the touch stream is a global
    5536             :          * gesture. No further events are sent to the clients from that
    5537             :          * particular gesture. Touch cancellation applies to all touch
    5538             :          * points currently active on this client's surface. The client is
    5539             :          * responsible for finalizing the touch points, future touch points
    5540             :          * on this surface may reuse the touch point ID.
    5541             :          *
    5542             :          * No frame event is required after the cancel event.
    5543             :          */
    5544             :         void (*cancel)(void *data,
    5545             :                        struct wl_touch *wl_touch);
    5546             :         /**
    5547             :          * update shape of touch point
    5548             :          *
    5549             :          * Sent when a touchpoint has changed its shape.
    5550             :          *
    5551             :          * This event does not occur on its own. It is sent before a
    5552             :          * wl_touch.frame event and carries the new shape information for
    5553             :          * any previously reported, or new touch points of that frame.
    5554             :          *
    5555             :          * Other events describing the touch point such as wl_touch.down,
    5556             :          * wl_touch.motion or wl_touch.orientation may be sent within the
    5557             :          * same wl_touch.frame. A client should treat these events as a
    5558             :          * single logical touch point update. The order of wl_touch.shape,
    5559             :          * wl_touch.orientation and wl_touch.motion is not guaranteed. A
    5560             :          * wl_touch.down event is guaranteed to occur before the first
    5561             :          * wl_touch.shape event for this touch ID but both events may occur
    5562             :          * within the same wl_touch.frame.
    5563             :          *
    5564             :          * A touchpoint shape is approximated by an ellipse through the
    5565             :          * major and minor axis length. The major axis length describes the
    5566             :          * longer diameter of the ellipse, while the minor axis length
    5567             :          * describes the shorter diameter. Major and minor are orthogonal
    5568             :          * and both are specified in surface-local coordinates. The center
    5569             :          * of the ellipse is always at the touchpoint location as reported
    5570             :          * by wl_touch.down or wl_touch.move.
    5571             :          *
    5572             :          * This event is only sent by the compositor if the touch device
    5573             :          * supports shape reports. The client has to make reasonable
    5574             :          * assumptions about the shape if it did not receive this event.
    5575             :          * @param id the unique ID of this touch point
    5576             :          * @param major length of the major axis in surface-local coordinates
    5577             :          * @param minor length of the minor axis in surface-local coordinates
    5578             :          * @since 6
    5579             :          */
    5580             :         void (*shape)(void *data,
    5581             :                       struct wl_touch *wl_touch,
    5582             :                       int32_t id,
    5583             :                       wl_fixed_t major,
    5584             :                       wl_fixed_t minor);

    5587             :          *
    5588             :          * Sent when a touchpoint has changed its orientation.
    5589             :          *

    5596             :          * wl_touch.frame. A client should treat these events as a single

    5600             :          * wl_touch.orientation event for this touch ID but both events may
    5601             :          * occur within the same wl_touch.frame.
    5602             :          *
    5603             :          * The orientation describes the clockwise angle of a touchpoint's
    5604             :          * major axis to the positive surface y-axis and is normalized to
    5605             :          * the -180 to +180 degree range. The granularity of orientation

    5612             :          * @param orientation angle between major axis and positive surface y-axis in degrees
    5613             :          * @since 6
    5614             :          */

    5820             : struct wl_output_listener {
    5821             :         /**
    5822             :          * properties of the output
    5823             :          *
    5824             :          * The geometry event describes geometric properties of the
    5825             :          * output. The event is sent when binding to the output object and
    5826             :          * whenever any of the properties change.
    5827             :          *
    5828             :          * The physical size can be set to zero if it doesn't make sense
    5829             :          * for this output (e.g. for projectors or virtual outputs).
    5830             :          *
    5831             :          * The geometry event will be followed by a done event (starting
    5832             :          * from version 2).
    5833             :          *
    5834             :          * Clients should use wl_surface.preferred_buffer_transform instead
    5835             :          * of the transform advertised by this event to find the preferred
    5836             :          * buffer transform to use for a surface.
    5837             :          *
    5838             :          * Note: wl_output only advertises partial information about the
    5839             :          * output position and identification. Some compositors, for
    5840             :          * instance those not implementing a desktop-style output layout or
    5841             :          * those exposing virtual outputs, might fake this information.
    5842             :          * Instead of using x and y, clients should use
    5843             :          * xdg_output.logical_position. Instead of using make and model,
    5844             :          * clients should use name and description.
    5845             :          * @param x x position within the global compositor space
    5846             :          * @param y y position within the global compositor space
    5847             :          * @param physical_width width in millimeters of the output
    5848             :          * @param physical_height height in millimeters of the output
    5849             :          * @param subpixel subpixel orientation of the output
    5850             :          * @param make textual description of the manufacturer
    5851             :          * @param model textual description of the model
    5852             :          * @param transform additional transformation applied to buffer contents during presentation
    5853             :          */
    5854             :         void (*geometry)(void *data,
    5855             :                          struct wl_output *wl_output,
    5856             :                          int32_t x,
    5857             :                          int32_t y,
    5858             :                          int32_t physical_width,
    5859             :                          int32_t physical_height,
    5860             :                          int32_t subpixel,
    5861             :                          const char *make,
    5862             :                          const char *model,
    5863             :                          int32_t transform);
    5864             :         /**
    5865             :          * advertise available modes for the output
    5866             :          *
    5867             :          * The mode event describes an available mode for the output.
    5868             :          *
    5869             :          * The event is sent when binding to the output object and there
    5870             :          * will always be one mode, the current mode. The event is sent
    5871             :          * again if an output changes mode, for the mode that is now
    5872             :          * current. In other words, the current mode is always the last
    5873             :          * mode that was received with the current flag set.
    5874             :          *
    5875             :          * Non-current modes are deprecated. A compositor can decide to
    5876             :          * only advertise the current mode and never send other modes.
    5877             :          * Clients should not rely on non-current modes.
    5878             :          *
    5879             :          * The size of a mode is given in physical hardware units of the
    5880             :          * output device. This is not necessarily the same as the output
    5881             :          * size in the global compositor space. For instance, the output
    5882             :          * may be scaled, as described in wl_output.scale, or transformed,
    5883             :          * as described in wl_output.transform. Clients willing to retrieve
    5884             :          * the output size in the global compositor space should use
    5885             :          * xdg_output.logical_size instead.
    5886             :          *
    5887             :          * The vertical refresh rate can be set to zero if it doesn't make
    5888             :          * sense for this output (e.g. for virtual outputs).
    5889             :          *
    5890             :          * The mode event will be followed by a done event (starting from
    5891             :          * version 2).
    5892             :          *
    5893             :          * Clients should not use the refresh rate to schedule frames.
    5894             :          * Instead, they should use the wl_surface.frame event or the
    5895             :          * presentation-time protocol.
    5896             :          *
    5897             :          * Note: this information is not always meaningful for all outputs.
    5898             :          * Some compositors, such as those exposing virtual outputs, might
    5899             :          * fake the refresh rate or the size.
    5900             :          * @param flags bitfield of mode flags
    5901             :          * @param width width of the mode in hardware units
    5902             :          * @param height height of the mode in hardware units
    5903             :          * @param refresh vertical refresh rate in mHz
    5904             :          */
    5905             :         void (*mode)(void *data,
    5906             :                      struct wl_output *wl_output,
    5907             :                      uint32_t flags,
    5908             :                      int32_t width,
    5909             :                      int32_t height,
    5910             :                      int32_t refresh);
    5911             :         /**
    5912             :          * sent all information about output
    5913             :          *
    5914             :          * This event is sent after all other properties have been sent
    5915             :          * after binding to the output object and after any other property
    5916             :          * changes done after that. This allows changes to the output
    5917             :          * properties to be seen as atomic, even if they happen via
    5918             :          * multiple events.
    5919             :          * @since 2
    5920             :          */
    5921             :         void (*done)(void *data,
    5922             :                      struct wl_output *wl_output);
    5923             :         /**
    5924             :          * output scaling properties
    5925             :          *
    5926             :          * This event contains scaling geometry information that is not
    5927             :          * in the geometry event. It may be sent after binding the output
    5928             :          * object or if the output scale changes later. The compositor will
    5929             :          * emit a non-zero, positive value for scale. If it is not sent,
    5930             :          * the client should assume a scale of 1.
    5931             :          *
    5932             :          * A scale larger than 1 means that the compositor will
    5933             :          * automatically scale surface buffers by this amount when
    5934             :          * rendering. This is used for very high resolution displays where
    5935             :          * applications rendering at the native resolution would be too
    5936             :          * small to be legible.
    5937             :          *
    5938             :          * Clients should use wl_surface.preferred_buffer_scale instead of
    5939             :          * this event to find the preferred buffer scale to use for a
    5940             :          * surface.
    5941             :          *
    5942             :          * The scale event will be followed by a done event.
    5943             :          * @param factor scaling factor of output
    5944             :          * @since 2
    5945             :          */
    5946             :         void (*scale)(void *data,
    5947             :                       struct wl_output *wl_output,
    5948             :                       int32_t factor);
    5949             :         /**
    5950             :          * name of this output
    5951             :          *
    5952             :          * Many compositors will assign user-friendly names to their
    5953             :          * outputs, show them to the user, allow the user to refer to an
    5954             :          * output, etc. The client may wish to know this name as well to
    5955             :          * offer the user similar behaviors.
    5956             :          *
    5957             :          * The name is a UTF-8 string with no convention defined for its
    5958             :          * contents. Each name is unique among all wl_output globals. The
    5959             :          * name is only guaranteed to be unique for the compositor
    5960             :          * instance.
    5961             :          *
    5962             :          * The same output name is used for all clients for a given
    5963             :          * wl_output global. Thus, the name can be shared across processes
    5964             :          * to refer to a specific wl_output global.
    5965             :          *
    5966             :          * The name is not guaranteed to be persistent across sessions,
    5967             :          * thus cannot be used to reliably identify an output in e.g.
    5968             :          * configuration files.
    5969             :          *
    5970             :          * Examples of names include 'HDMI-A-1', 'WL-1', 'X11-1', etc.
    5971             :          * However, do not assume that the name is a reflection of an
    5972             :          * underlying DRM connector, X11 connection, etc.
    5973             :          *
    5974             :          * The name event is sent after binding the output object. This
    5975             :          * event is only sent once per output object, and the name does not
    5976             :          * change over the lifetime of the wl_output global.
    5977             :          *
    5978             :          * Compositors may re-use the same output name if the wl_output
    5979             :          * global is destroyed and re-created later. Compositors should
    5980             :          * avoid re-using the same name if possible.
    5981             :          *
    5982             :          * The name event will be followed by a done event.
    5983             :          * @param name output name
    5984             :          * @since 4
    5985             :          */
    5986             :         void (*name)(void *data,
    5987             :                      struct wl_output *wl_output,
    5988             :                      const char *name);
    5989             :         /**
    5990             :          * human-readable description of this output
    5991             :          *
    5992             :          * Many compositors can produce human-readable descriptions of
    5993             :          * their outputs. The client may wish to know this description as
    5994             :          * well, e.g. for output selection purposes.
    5995             :          *
    5996             :          * The description is a UTF-8 string with no convention defined for
    5997             :          * its contents. The description is not guaranteed to be unique
    5998             :          * among all wl_output globals. Examples might include 'Foocorp 11"
    5999             :          * Display' or 'Virtual X11 output via :1'.
    6000             :          *
    6001             :          * The description event is sent after binding the output object
    6002             :          * and whenever the description changes. The description is

    6238             :  *
    6239             :  * The to-be sub-surface must not already have another role, and it
    6240             :  * must not have an existing wl_subsurface object. Otherwise the
    6241             :  * bad_surface protocol error is raised.
    6242             :  *
    6243             :  * Adding sub-surfaces to a parent is a double-buffered operation on the
    6244             :  * parent (see wl_surface.commit). The effect of adding a sub-surface
    6245             :  * becomes visible on the next time the state of the parent surface is
    6246             :  * applied.
    6247             :  *
    6248             :  * The parent surface must not be one of the child surface's descendants,
    6249             :  * and the parent must be different from the child surface, otherwise the
    6250             :  * bad_parent protocol error is raised.
    6251             :  *
    6252             :  * This request modifies the behaviour of wl_surface.commit request on
    6253             :  * the sub-surface, see the documentation on wl_subsurface interface.

    6374             :  * The reference surface must be one of the sibling surfaces, or the
    6375             :  * parent surface. Using any other surface, including this sub-surface,
    6376             :  * will cause a protocol error.
    6377             :  *
    6378             :  * The z-order is double-buffered. Requests are handled in order and
    6379             :  * applied immediately to a pending state. The final pending state is
    6380             :  * copied to the active state the next time the state of the parent
    6381             :  * surface is applied.
    6382             :  *
    6383             :  * A new sub-surface is initially added as the top-most in the stack
    6384             :  * of its siblings and parent.

    6406             : /**
    6407             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_subsurface
    6408             :  *
    6409             :  * Change the commit behaviour of the sub-surface to synchronized
    6410             :  * mode, also described as the parent dependent mode.
    6411             :  *
    6412             :  * In synchronized mode, wl_surface.commit on a sub-surface will
    6413             :  * accumulate the committed state in a cache, but the state will
    6414             :  * not be applied and hence will not change the compositor output.
    6415             :  * The cached state is applied to the sub-surface immediately after
    6416             :  * the parent surface's state is applied. This ensures atomic
    6417             :  * updates of the parent and all its synchronized sub-surfaces.
    6418             :  * Applying the cached state will invalidate the cache, so further
    6419             :  * parent surface commits do not (re-)apply old state.
    6420             :  *
    6421             :  * See wl_subsurface for the recursive effect of this mode.
    6422             :  */
    6423             : static inline void
    6424             : wl_subsurface_set_sync(struct wl_subsurface *wl_subsurface)
    6425             : {
    6426             :         wl_proxy_marshal_flags((struct wl_proxy *) wl_subsurface,
    6427             :                          WL_SUBSURFACE_SET_SYNC, NULL, wl_proxy_get_version((struct wl_proxy *) wl_subsurface), 0);
    6428             : }
    6429             : 
    6430             : /**
    6431             :  * @ingroup iface_wl_subsurface
    6432             :  *
    6433             :  * Change the commit behaviour of the sub-surface to desynchronized
    6434             :  * mode, also described as independent or freely running mode.
    6435             :  *
    6436             :  * In desynchronized mode, wl_surface.commit on a sub-surface will
    6437             :  * apply the pending state directly, without caching, as happens
    6438             :  * normally with a wl_surface. Calling wl_surface.commit on the
    6439             :  * parent surface has no effect on the sub-surface's wl_surface
    6440             :  * state. This mode allows a sub-surface to be updated on its own.
    6441             :  *
    6442             :  * If cached state exists when wl_surface.commit is called in
    6443             :  * desynchronized mode, the pending state is added to the cached
    6444             :  * state, and applied as a whole. This invalidates the cache.
    6445             :  *
    6446             :  * Note: even if a sub-surface is set to desynchronized, a parent
    6447             :  * sub-surface may override it to behave as synchronized. For details,
    6448             :  * see wl_subsurface.
    6449             :  *
    6450             :  * If a surface's parent surface behaves as desynchronized, then
    6451             :  * the cached state is applied on set_desync.
    6452             :  */
    6453             : static inline void

    6455             : {

    6457             :                          WL_SUBSURFACE_SET_DESYNC, NULL, wl_proxy_get_version((struct wl_proxy *) wl_subsurface), 0);
    6458           0 : }
